Build distinction: unit/race targets use untagged tests by default, timeout2m and cpu4 for race. build-tagged uses `go test -run NONE`, so it can compile and initialize packages without running named tests; it is not purely syntax analysis. BUILD_TAGS contains integration while integration target selects TestInteg_ with10m timeout; those are different from unit flags. Snapshot CI runs only TestCheckSnapshot, narrowing earlier shared-file update/check concern for that exact command; generic `go test -tags snapshot` still selects updater tests unless filtered. Top-level generate explicitly invokes snapshot updating. No Makefile/workflow/test ran. `make` parsing itself has a mktemp shell expansion, another reason reading is distinct from executing. Default repo tools/golint use latest; actions are version tags, Codegen JDK uses moving latest, and ci-find-smithy can clone a matching fork branch and modreplace across modules. Hence CI definitions alone do not prove tests ran against this pinned dependency or reproducible bytes. Exact runner/tool/branch receipts remain external.

AWS utility subtree7/7 files817 lines source-complete: copy113/test359, equal34/test39, prettify132, string_value89/test51. Copy is reflective and selective, not a universal immutable snapshot: readers are deliberately shared (test232–251 reads copy then expects original exhausted), unexported fields omitted, nil source maps/slices skipped rather than clearing existing destination, interfaces/arrays fall to direct assignability, slices allocate source capacity rather than only length. Recursive pointers/maps/slices have no cycle/depth/resource budget; arbitrary mismatched destinations can encounter reflect preconditions before scalar assignability guards, so “incompatible fields ignored” prose is not a blanket panic-free API. Root parameter is propagated but has no conditional use. CopyOf requires a pointer type and allocates its element. No reflection helper ran.

Positive Copy tests mutate copied exported pointers/maps/slices and assert originals differ, preserving genuine independence for those fixtures. Nested exported-field assertion167 compares f2.B.B with itself, so it cannot validate copying from f1; other tests compare real source/destination fields. Nil tests use initially empty destinations, not stale-nonnil overwrite. DeepEqual dereferences one top-level layer and special-cases string aliases, retaining nil-type distinction; tests cover those semantics, not cryptographic identity/evidence equality. Prettify omits nil/unexported fields and formats byte slices as length only, without sensitive-tag checks; StringValue checks `sensitive:true` on struct fields, but not arbitrary root values/map keys or all formatting APIs. Exact fixture proves tagged byte field redaction for that path only. Neither formatter has cycle/depth/output bounds or deterministic map-key sorting. Thus printable output and copied models can differ materially from original complete evidence, without any actual secret exposure being claimed.

SDK testing subtree7/7 files907 lines fully source-read: assert223/test90, util202/test76, discard12, endless_reader13 and certificate_utils291. AssertURL records Host/Scheme/Path mismatch via t.Error yet returns only AssertQuery's boolean, ignores userinfo/fragment/raw escaped path and normalizes query through URL.Query().Encode. Thus boolean alone is weaker than t status; no accepting-bad-URL claim for tests checking t failure. AssertQuery first checks equal key sets, then rewrites only expected numbered-map keys, deleting *all keys with prefix* and recreating sorted `.key` entries without their `.value` associations. It then compares surviving expected values, not the deleted entries. This conditional map-value blind spot requires a real caller/fixture; bounded search within exact core/S3 found only helper tests for exported AssertQuery/AssertXML, not a production/service caller. Keep it separate from Smithy testing's different URL-form helper. JSON uses whole-input Unmarshal into a map, preserving full-input rejection but normalizing ordering/numeric/duplicates and not arbitrary root arrays. XML reuses the supplied container in both interfaces and projects by its schema; fixture declares `entries` while supplied XML uses `entry`, so nested-map equivalence cannot be credited as complete nonempty map validation. Exact standard parser behavior/actual pointer alias outcomes remain unexecuted dependency questions. Tests' positive reordered-JSON and negative ordinary-query controls remain explicit; no tests ran.

ZeroReader fills zeros; EndlessReader only reports len(p), leaving bytes unchanged and never EOF; DiscardAt reports successful length without persistence. ReadCloser tracks a Boolean close, synthetic fill and remaining size, with final bytes+EOF; its tests verify count/flag semantics, not OS handle release or all resource leaks. FakeContext is an unconstrained stub, not full context cancellation semantics. StashEnv/PopEnv clear process environment; mock credentials only invoke callbacks. ParseSigV4Signature splits header text with unchecked credential indexes and retains label prefixes in parsed segments; it neither verifies signature cryptography nor validates grammar. Any test using it proves argument/header shape only, and malformed input needs a caller boundary before impact. No headers/credentials/environment were collected or helpers imported.

Certificate helper init generates a root and two RSA4096 leaf pairs, so even package initialization is active cryptographic work; none was invoked. Templates use loopback IP SANs, one-year local validity and fixed serial42; these are synthetic testing identities, not public trust. NewTLSClientCertServer returns an *unstarted* server configured RequireAndVerifyClientCert, not a running authenticated endpoint. Temporary-file helpers do not unwind earlier files on later failure, do not close/remove on write error and ignore deferred Close error; cleanup stops at first remove error. CreateTLSServer releases a probed ephemeral port before rebinding in a goroutine (no reservation), returns no shutdown handle, and readiness loop accepts any non-connection-refused error or exhausts60 iterations then returns nil error. It does not prove successful trusted TLS/application response; no server, TLS, file write/removal, network probe, signing or cert generation ran here. These are helper lifecycle limits, not a reproduced deployed leak.

SDK middleware/timing/user-agent continuation13 files1542 lines complete: middleware.go169/test190, OS adapters25/25, internal rand34/test39, internal sdk time75/test33/interfaces10, timeconv14, sdkio13, user_agent399/test516. ClientRequestID uses a supplied-module random reader and UUID formatting, replacing its canonical header slice with one invocation ID. Fixed-zero fixture verifies the exact UUID/header and wrong-transport rejection, not entropy/uniqueness/server correlation. RecordResponseTiming records sdk.NowTime *after next returns*, including time spent in downstream deserialization; the label responseAt is not necessarily socket-arrival time. It parses supplied Date header for serverTime and computes difference without correcting this PC's clock. Missing/invalid Date only omits skew and optionally logs; metadata is recorded even on error/no raw response. Exact synthetic time/skew tests are positive controls, not trusted remote time. AddRawResponse retains the object reference, not an immutable captured body or automatically persisted archive.

NewRequestUserAgent includes SDK/version, normalized OS, Go version, architecture and optional execution-environment label; metadata methods actually append to User-Agent, while X-Amz-User-Agent emission is commented out despite constructor prose. Credential-source features identify categories, not credential secrets or proven exercised service capabilities; several constants explicitly mark unsupported/not-implemented features, so enumeration is not a capability catalog. Features deduplicate and sort; strings.Map replaces disallowed runes with '-', a lossy representation. updateHTTPHeader preserves only the first existing canonical-key value and replaces slice with combined string; other header values are dropped, and direct map access differs from canonicalizing Header.Get. No privacy/anonymity claim follows. Tests assert exact headers including existing prefix, environment labels, sanitation, feature order/dedup and full stack insertion. They deliberately clear/rebuild process environment through clearEnv; none ran, and no PC environment was accessed/changed. Missing tests for multi-valued headers, arbitrary feature strings or concurrent middleware mutation do not establish exploitability.

Internal randomness defaults to crypto/rand but is replaceable for tests; exact floor/ceiling Float64 tests use deterministic bytes, not entropy proof. Timing hooks are mutable globals; TestingUseNopSleep/ReferenceTime restore defaults, not the previously installed hooks, so nested/custom-hook ownership must be coordinated. Sleep stops timer and selects cancellation; tests cover ordinary/canceled contexts without proving all scheduling races. FloatSecondsDur uses unchecked float-to-duration arithmetic with no local finite/range guard; actual caller constraints remain open. Invalidator is only a method interface, not cancellation/revocation semantics. Modern vs pre-Go1.16 OS helpers have build constraints; old Darwin→other behavior is not selected on the supported Go1.24+ path.

Complete concrete waiter caller `api_op_HeadBucket.go`1–740 narrows helper reachability. Both waiter constructors default5s/120s; per-call options are copied then callbacks applied. Default BucketExists: nil operation error means success, typed NotFound means retry, other errors propagate. Default BucketNotExists: typed NotFound means success (possibly nil output), nil error means retry, other errors propagate. Thus a successful Wait does not mean a resource was created/deleted by this tool, nor grant broader access; it means the chosen state predicate accepted a response. Generic403 is not treated as absence success. Loop tracks request/classifier elapsed and planned sleeps with no explicit maximum attempt count; transport-level retries happen inside each operation. LogWaitAttempts copies the APIOptions slice before append, then user ClientOptions apply after base/API options and can modify them. No request, sleep, signer, provider or waiter ran here. Wait scheduling and observability finite source closures: `waiter/`3/3 files249 lines, `rand/`4/4 files172 lines, `logging/`2/2 files155 lines, plus endpoints/endpoint24, deprecated root document11 and doc3. ComputeDelay is an arithmetic/jitter helper, not a polling loop or service-state proof. Nonpositive attempts and remaining time return zero before validation; min/max validation checks equality to zero, not negative values or min>max. Last-attempt adjustment can produce negative remainingTime-minDelay when positive remaining time is below the minimum. That is not an observed negative sleep: concrete caller guards are required. Bounded HeadBucket search shows caller checks remainingTime<MinDelay or<=0 before ComputeDelay, and rejects MinDelay>MaxDelay, narrowing that concern for those routes; the entire HeadBucket file is not yet read/credited, and no universal caller guarantee follows. They do not test negative inputs or real service convergence.

rand.Reader defaults to crypto/rand.Reader but is publicly replaceable; CryptoRandInt63n delegates through it rather than guaranteeing an untampered source. UUID constructor uses its supplied reader, io.ReadFull16 bytes and explicit version/variant bits. GetBytes returns its allocated partially filled buffer alongside read error; callers must honor the error. Exact zero/one-vector UUID tests prove formatting/bit masks for those cases, not entropy; benchmark explicitly uses math/rand. None ran and no random/crypto operation was invoked. StandardLogger delegates to log.Printf with SDK prefix and classification, without local redaction/level filtering. Tests match substrings using unanchored regex against an accumulating buffer, not a complete log transcript; context forwarding is separately asserted. Nop does nothing, so logger installation alone does not establish evidence retention. Endpoint URI/headers/properties are mutable transport configuration data, not proof of identity, ownership or connectivity. The old root Document interface is explicitly deprecated in favor of the document package, preventing interface-name equivalence assumptions.

RingBuffer retains only the latest buffer-length unread bytes, overwriting oldest on full writes; Bytes reads a value-copy of indexes, preserving original unread state, while Reset clears indexes but retains backing bytes without zeroization. Constructor aliases the supplied slice; there is no lock. Nonempty writes with zero-length backing storage reach index0 without a capacity guard. This is a conditional API precondition concern, not demonstrated remote input control. Its DeserializationError snapshot is the last1024 consumed bytes, not necessarily the response tail/full body or wire capture. It converts io.EOF to nil after the document helper; root/body completeness depends on that helper, not yet fully reviewed here. Reset tests use EqualFold, not case-exact evidence. No zero-capacity write, aliasing/concurrency, Bytes preservation or backing-byte-erasure oracle appears. Full Smithy `errors.go`1–138, `validation.go`1–141 and `sync/error.go`1–54 clarify evidence handling. DeserializationError exposes Snapshot separately; Error() includes the wrapped error text but does not format Snapshot. API fault categories and service/operation labels are data wrappers, not policy decisions. InvalidParamsError collects caller-supplied validation errors; it does not validate models itself. AddNested mutates nested error objects through their interfaces; Errs copies the slice, not the referenced error objects, so callers should not assume immutable independent histories. OnceErr uses a mutex to record the first nonnil error and closes its signal channel once; constructor initialization is required for a usable channel. This proves first-error coordination, not complete error aggregation, task cancellation or cleanup.

Request-compression flow source-complete4/4 files314 lines: `private/requestcompression/request_compression.go`1–104, `middleware_capture_request_compression.go`1–53, `gzip.go`1–31 and tests1–126. A Serialize-stage registration appends compression; first recognized algorithm wins (only gzip registered), disabled mode bypasses even configuration checks, invalid minimum thresholds otherwise reject outside0–10485760. This 10MiB constant bounds the configurable *minimum*, not input/output memory or compressed body size. Unknown-length streams and streams below threshold skip compression; gzip uses io.ReadAll then a second buffer, without context checks or an input limit. Source establishes resource policy absence here, not an observed exhaustion. Successful replacement sets the request stream and appends gzip to an existing Content-Encoding, preserving layered encoding intent rather than inspecting whether the old body matches that header. Failures after consuming input have no local rewind/rollback; retry behavior requires caller handling. Capture middleware inserts before the first RequestCompression ID, copies the full uncompressed stream into the provided buffer then rewinds; it neither limits nor redacts the captured body. A nil stream/buffer is not guarded here, with actual caller preconditions still needed.

Compression tests retain decompressed payload equality and complete header-map equality for four happy/skip cases. However the body assertion is guarded by `if stream != nil`, so unexpected absence can evade that assertion; SetStream error is discarded in setup. No unknown-length, erroring-reader, threshold-out-of-range, unknown-algorithm or capture/rewind failure cases occur in this file. Tests construct gzip locally and inspect bytes; none ran here, and no request or decompression experiment occurred. Bearer continuation, full existing `auth/bearer/`6/6 files959 lines: `token.go`1–51, `middleware.go`1–105, `docs.go`1–4, `token_cache.go`1–209, `middleware_test.go`1–78 and `token_cache_test.go`1–512. Token expiration is local metadata (CanExpire=false suppresses expiry checks), not introspection/revocation. Static providers return their token unchanged. Authentication retrieves then decorates; SignHTTPSMessage checks HTTP request type and HTTPS, clones and replaces Authorization. It does not itself validate token expiration, emptiness, audience or service authorization. Header tests use synthetic abc123 and exact header equality plus wrong-type/plain-HTTP rejection; they do not establish TLS handshake, certificate validation or service acceptance. No credential or request was used here.

Cache state is one atomic token per instance and fixed refresh keys, not keyed by tenant/context/audience. Shared cache/provider lifetime is therefore an integration precondition; no concrete multi-identity misuse is established. A fresh nonempty cached token returns without consulting caller cancellation. Initial/expired retrieval blocks; early refresh normally returns the existing token while a deduplicated refresh runs. Async failures are ignored by that caller and retain the old cache; minimum-delay timestamp is set on failure and reset to zero on success (not an unconditional delay between every attempt). The timeout wraps the provider context; a provider ignoring cancellation can still block, so the option is not a hard execution kill. singleRetrieve stores and returns any provider token without checking expiry/empty value; the next cache lookup rejects an empty Value. There is no invalidation/revocation method in this file. A canceled waiter can return while the provider later populates the cache; this is not token revocation.

Full `context/suppress_expired.go`1–82 confirms refresh suppresses deadline/cancellation by embedding Background, while context values disappear after original cancellation unless preservation was enabled before wrapping. Provider authority derived from context must account for this behavior. Full Smithy `internal/sync/singleflight/`4/4 files570 lines (implementation211, tests321, docs9, BSD license29) confirms mutex-protected duplicate suppression, one buffered channel per waiter, no waiter removal on cancellation, no channel closure, and no bound on retained waiters while a provider blocks. Forget allows a new flight without canceling the old one; a forgotten old flight cannot delete the new map entry. Provider panic with channel waiters deliberately triggers an unrecoverable goroutine panic rather than ordinary returned error; Goexit takes a separate path without channel result. These are read-only source deductions, not exercised crashes/hangs. Fork docs pin upstream provenance67f06af15bc961c363a7260195bcd53487529a21; that provenance does not make its exact fork bytes interchangeable with SDK core's separate copy.

Tests retain strong exact token and provider-call-count controls, cancellation-versus-provider-continuation behavior, provider timeout response, and async failure/minimum-delay/blocking-refresh cases. Concurrent cache test warms the cache before parallel calls, so it does not prove simultaneous cold retrieval behavior. Singleflight Do suppression test accepts any call count from1 through9 for10 callers, not exactly one; Forget regression requires a third caller to receive the second flight's result. Crash tests intentionally spawn subprocesses and check both failure and panic marker, but none ran. Bearer tests' helper waits via the async key and may run its own no-op when no flight exists; asynchronous refresh is demand-driven, not a periodic scheduler. **Caller correction, complete middleware tree:** full `step_initialize.go`1–300, `step_serialize.go`1–306, `step_build.go`1–299 and `step_finalize.go`1–299 reveal linked decorators, not orderedIDs. Complete `middleware/ordered_group.go`1–271 and `ordered_group_test.go`1–244: public group Add/Insert reject empty IDs and existing IDs; Swap permits replacement using the same ID but rejects collision with another ID. Ordering mutations return errors before changing the item map. List copies the underlying order; GetOrder builds an ordered item slice. REST/compression/reader helpers complete rest232/gzip28/reader40/errors16/doc11. Final auth-selection bound for the Express identity mismatch: reread complete `endpoint_auth_resolver.go`1–127 and `auth.go`20–270. Default endpoint resolver options are authoritative, normalize Express IDs and append anonymous; scheme selection accepts the first supported option with a nonnil identity resolver, subject to explicit preferences. CreateSession's operation binder forces ordinary V4, and the nested copied client options provide its resolver. Identity retrieval errors stop the call rather than trying later options, so appended anonymous is not evidence of fallback after credential denial. Static nonnil client provider A therefore bounds the earlier A-versus-B mismatch; nil/anonymous/custom scheme preferences or custom resolver/middleware are separate cases. Endpoint resolution occurs in both auth resolution and later URI middleware, so a stateful custom resolver can return differing results; default fixtures do not validate such a custom implementation. No runtime auth negotiation or session API exercised. Accessible continuation priority: remaining exact SDK configuration/cancellation tests and operation/customization tests, preserving these conditional findings and contrary evidence.

Reread `auth.go`295–345 and `options.go`200–252: identity resolution delegates using those copied options and their Credentials provider. Therefore, under static default-client credentials A and per-operation override B, the default Express provider keys the cache using B but the nested CreateSession path still resolves A. Full Smithy cache interface20/LRU70/test94 closes the direct cache dependency. Capacity100 is enforced by eviction at equality for normal positive capacity; Get and replacement move entries to the front. The LRU itself is not thread-safe, but the Express wrapper locks both Get and Put, contrary to a simple default unprotected-cache race claim. Zero-capacity construction would reach empty eviction, but Express fixes capacity100, so this is not a reachable default issue. Tests cover positive-capacity ordering and replacement cardinality, not concurrency or zero capacity. The generated CreateSession comments describe service permission/expiration contracts; they were read as pinned documentation, not verified live capabilities. Existing serializers/deserializers/validators were already read; no new service request or account access occurred. Continue exact auth resolver selection and caller tests to bound the mismatch.

Express credential flow, full eight existing files: `express.go`10, `express_default.go`171, `express_resolve.go`40, `express_user_agent.go`44, `express_test.go`311 and customization `express.go`45/`express_config.go`19/`express_properties.go`22. Exact hashes in queue. Default provider shares a mutex-protected LRU cache, nominal capacity100, with clones but does not share their singleflight group. Per-operation finalization always clones the default provider with operation credentials; duplicate suppression is consequently per clone rather than necessarily across concurrent operations. Cache key includes HMAC of base access ID/secret plus bucket, omitting session token, endpoint and region. These omissions require caller/authority analysis before alleging cache isolation failure. Each Retrieve first fetches base credentials; a valid cached session returns while refresh is triggered asynchronously inside one minute of expiry. Unlike generic credential cache, the first caller's context is passed into CreateSession without suppression; canceled leader effects on shared refresh remain conditional source behavior. Cached response checks only required nonnil fields, not nonempty values or future expiration.

Important unresolved binding: CloneWithBaseCredentials changes `v4creds` used for cache keying but retains `p.client`. The retrieve method calls that client's CreateSession without per-call credential options. Follow the complete operation/caller before concluding whether operation override credentials authenticate that session request. The operation-override test replaces CreateSession with a mock whose method ignores context, input and options; it confirms separate cache entries and call counts, not which credentials signed CreateSession. Other tests check two-bucket reuse and async refresh result counts, not LRU eviction, leader cancellation, clone-wide suppression or token-only changes. Tests replace global sdk.NowTime without restoring it in this file; source only, this PC's clock/config were never changed. Express identity properties fall back to context bucket only when absent, reject empty bucket, and wrap provider errors with formatting rather than `%w`. User-agent feature checks only the bucket suffix and is not auth evidence. No tests, providers, session APIs or credentials executed. Next existing CreateSession/LRU dependencies.

V4 middleware/test pair complete: `aws/signer/v4/middleware.go` 1–421 (SHA-256 `03e6c9cff9f6f9e40c5f4b12e4c7d892722e185fdfb3e4db2faeb18a49b57aa7`) and `middleware_test.go` 1–472 (SHA-256 `707c475213cc8a9904efc23a838f79baae794a9671f43bf3d51b2f14cf5107be`). Dynamic payload selection checks request HTTPS classification before dispatching unsigned versus compute middleware; the shown tests merely parse HTTP/HTTPS URLs, without TLS handshakes. An existing context hash bypasses computation. Computation drains and rewinds the stream with no local length/deadline bound and calls span.End both explicitly and by defer on success, subject to the provider distinction above. Read/seek errors stop before next. This is not checksum validation by the service or proof of actual transmitted bytes.

The exported SignHTTPRequestMiddleware is explicitly deprecated and documented as replaced by per-service auth-scheme signing; do not mistake its current-time/no-skew call for the active S3 adapter already reviewed. It skips nil/anonymous providers, requires a context payload hash, retrieves credentials, applies path-escaping context override, calls signer and stores signing credentials. GetSignedRequestSignature extracts hex bytes from header/query; it does not verify authenticity. Its header search uses substring position only as a presence check but slices from fixed offset, leaving malformed prefixed fields as a parser question rather than a verification bypass. Current tests cover ordinary signature fields/whitespace/empty/missing cases, not prefixed fields or query extraction.

Oracle limits: error type checks in payload/sign tests pass an interface variable to errors.As, which does not specifically prove the expected concrete wrapper type. Fake signer checks arguments/logger and emits the test name, not canonical request or cryptographic output. No explicit called-count oracle proves signer bypass for nil/anonymous cases. Stack ordering tests compare IDs, not behavior of a transported request. No upstream test, stream read, TLS, credential retrieval or signing executed. Remaining helper, functional-test and stream-signing queue stays active.

Ordinary V4 signer complete: `aws/signer/v4/v4.go` 1–565 (SHA-256 `2610f74d556baaf502b9a640f63fc81aaa1d98a1a00775cbac26e458609e0704`) and `v4_test.go` 1–368 (SHA-256 `59e31c00998e0310c60c3bddf90de65766325a476890f473d8cc21aa503ec4aa`). Header signing modifies the supplied request; ordinary V4 presigning explicitly clones it before Build, unlike the separately reviewed V4a presign implementation. Do not generalize mutation behavior across both. Payload hash is supplied and trusted, with no body verification in this layer. Query values sort, Request.Host overrides URL.Host for canonical host, positive ContentLength is synthesized, duplicate case-insensitive header names merge, values are normalized and paths escaped unless disabled. No destination authorization or transport validation occurs in the signer. Expiration is deliberately left to callers. Context is used for logging, not a local cancellation check. Enabled signing logs contain canonical request/string-to-sign and full presigned URL without redaction here.

Test oracles include fixed expected HMAC authorization/query signatures, query sorting and exact canonical whitespace normalization; body identity is checked without reading/replacing it in SignHTTP. The helper computes a payload hash by consuming its reader before signing, so these fixtures are not proof of transmitted body integrity. Presence-only returned-header checks are weaker than exact values. No upstream signing/tests were run.

Full derived-key cache `aws/signer/internal/v4/cache.go` 1–116 (SHA-256 `8d91dbc613c88a0c124fff1c8172fa4a70ffd74e35d2103f5c756e1f56939331`): mutex-protected cache indexes region/service, then validates AccessKeyID and calendar day; secret/token/expiry changes with the same ID are not cache-key inputs. Service token is separately placed in canonical fields, so omission from derivation alone does not show unsigned token substitution. A same-ID changed-secret stale-key scenario depends on provider behavior and signer reuse; it is not an observed credential compromise. Cache has no eviction/cardinality bound in this file and returns stored key bytes without copying; region/service are caller configuration, not demonstrated attacker inputs. Next follow existing internal helper/functional tests and middleware lifetimes. Flagged operation remains stopped.

Credential cache implementation/test flow: complete SDK core `aws/credentials.go` 1–235, `credential_cache.go` 1–236, `credential_cache_test.go` 1–670, `aws/context.go` 1–23 and `internal/sync/singleflight/singleflight.go` 1–211; exact hashes/ranges in the ledger. Cached nonexpired keys return without consulting the provider or caller cancellation; expiry is wall-clock based only when CanExpire is true. HasKeys checks presence, not correctness, ownership or IAM. Refresh is singleflight per cache instance and uses a context that suppresses Deadline/Done/Err while retaining values. A canceled caller can return while shared refresh continues: cancellation of waiting is separate from cancellation of provider work. No local provider deadline is imposed here. Provider-specific refresh-failure and expiry-adjustment strategies can replace results, while the default returns refresh errors. Invalidation only stores nil; an already-running refresh can later store its result, so it is not demonstrated to be a revocation barrier. This is a local cache-lifetime observation, not a claim that revoked credentials are accepted by a service.

Singleflight holds one in-flight function per key and a buffered result channel per waiter; canceled cache callers do not remove their result channels from the shared call. Those channels remain retained until completion, without an explicit waiter bound here. Panic behavior intentionally re-panics, including an unrecoverable goroutine panic for channel callers; do not treat provider panics as ordinary returned errors. Goexit has a separate path. Cache tests cover reuse, time expiry, jitter windows, refresh/adjust strategies and concurrent retrieval using synthetic providers. The mock expiry-adjuster declares `expectDur` but never compares its `dur` argument (610–618); returned-credential assertions do not independently validate that argument. Tests lack invalidation-during-refresh and canceled waiter lifetime oracles in this file. The named race test uses randomized scheduling but source reading is not a race-detector run. No providers, tests or credentials were executed/retrieved. Continue the existing singleflight and credential tests before generalizing concurrency behavior.

POST policy flow complete: `presign_post.go` 1–420 (SHA-256 `00e30b2b46548a36b8b528233712f435eb158f5f92caf416bddb7b96114c9210`) and `presign_post_test.go` 1–536 (SHA-256 `8cc94c3fc00822ededf95f6a61219c974a47979738b91c117182640c249a06e8`). The entry point reuses PutObject setup with a no-op HTTP client and substitutes a finalize middleware returning URL/form fields without calling next. It retrieves configured ordinary credentials, assigns skew-adjusted time, creates JSON/base64 policy and HMAC-derived signature. This is local form construction, not object upload or evidence of a permitted service effect. No upstream code or crypto was executed in this review.

Source distinctions: unlike URL conversion, POST conversion only defaults zero expiration and has no negative-duration guard in this complete file; it can construct a past expiration, without establishing service acceptance or useful privilege. `PresignPostOptions.ClientOptions` is declared, but the entry point appends inherited `clientOptions.ClientOptions` and does not consume the per-call `options.ClientOptions`. Extra policy conditions are appended rather than reconciled; default exact key restriction is omitted only when recognized `[]any` starts-with/key or map forms exist. Recognition checks shape/presence, not condition validity; `[]string` starts-with is JSON-serializable but not recognized by that guard, leaving the default exact-key condition. These are trusted caller configuration and policy-generation questions, not an authorization bypass. The default adapter accepts signer options but does not apply them, so passing logging options does not itself establish logged signature material here.

Test evidence: fixed time plus a precomputed signature and exact base64 policy provide a stronger local regression oracle than mere field presence. The source comment explicitly says its capitalization differs from the public documentation vector; do not call this the unchanged official vector. Synthetic example credentials remain fixture data and were never used. Integration-shaped cases check exact URL and decoded expiration, but only nonempty form fields; error/nil checks use nonfatal errors before later dereferences. The condition-collision fixture checks that both default and extra conditions survive, not whether the service accepts their conjunction. `findInSlice` returns only the first map match, so its bucket assertion does not exclude contradictory later conditions; `isPresent` supplies separate positive extra-condition coverage. Tests cover default/override expiration, body-ignored intent, custom path-style endpoints, MRAP URL shape and key override; none redeem forms, verify service permissions, or exercise per-call ClientOptions/negative-expiry behavior. Existing wider SDK queue remains open.

V4a crypto helper closure (source only): compare.go1–31/compare_test.go1–61 and ecc.go1–114/ecc_test.go1–278 are complete, per-file hashes retained in the queue. Equal-length byte comparison traverses all bytes; its benchmark compares two fixed inputs and is not timing-side-channel proof. Key helpers delegate scalar multiplication and ECDSA verification to Go crypto. VerifySignature ignores ASN.1 remainder, so it does not itself require total signature-input consumption; actual production caller reachability remains to inspect. HMAC KDF checks large output/counter bounds but has no negative-length guard; the reviewed production derivation supplies fixed256 bits, contrary to claiming attacker-controlled allocation on that path. Tests cover fixed P256/P384 coordinates, randomized sign/verify round-trips, a flipped signature, and one fixed ASN.1 signature/public-key/message vector; no malformed ASN.1 remainder/missing-integer or KDF boundary oracle is present here. A test named known signing value generates a fresh signature, whereas the final format test uses a fixed signature: labels alone are not evidence. No tests or timing measurements ran.

Fully read `src/net/http/internal/chunked.go`1–301, SHA256 `d2a088e8d1d0ae6ff593b27011982ef9115fee593cb57cc97eb34982ff17f5e2`; exact bytes and contiguous ranges are in [the standard-library ledger](stdlib-queue.json). Lines19–86 and151–211 enforce a4096-byte chunk-line bound, mandatory CRLF with no earlier CR, at most16 hexadecimal size digits, and cumulative excessive-overhead accounting. Extensions are stripped at the first semicolon without validating their grammar; this is an explicit implementation TODO, not proof of an exploitable parser disagreement. The overhead allowance is not a response-body size cap. The size arithmetic converts uint64 to int64 and multiplies by two; extreme-size interpretation remains a source question requiring the consuming reader and regression tests, not a demonstrated allocation or remote effect.

Lines88–149 return available data rather than waiting for another header/footer when possible, verify each chunk's trailing CRLF, and convert premature EOF into UnexpectedEOF. A zero chunk produces the framing reader's EOF; the outer transfer reader owns trailer processing. Thus chunk EOF alone is neither application checksum verification nor authenticated service completion.

Lines214–301 emit one chunk per nonempty Write, detect a short payload write, propagate delimiter/flush errors, and let Close emit only `0\r\n`. The caller must emit trailers and their final CRLF. Close does not close the underlying writer. The historical comment about Conn.Write is not independent evidence about the current server implementation, which remains unread. Exact chunk regression tests and shared test harness are next dependencies.

## Transport regression source completion and finite fourteen-file milestone — 2026-10-18

Final contiguous transport_test.go7041–8107 read; whole1–8107 complete. Broken HTTP2 connection test7013–7073 injects failures after TLS handshake and expects five dials but only one successful GotConn. Concurrent HTTP2 accounting7075–7126 checks300 requests under a one-connection cap. Alternate cancellation7128–7154 uses a synthetic protocol; rewind7160–7179 checks only nonzero body before and after ErrSkipAltProtocol, not exact byte equality. Signed Content-Length7181–7200 requires nil response plus an error substring. Race helper7202–7298 uses fake connections and1000 finite10000-byte requests; its oracle primarily depends on race detector/termination, not returned content or error classification.

Shared-connection cancellation7300–7387 coordinates callback gates to ensure canceling the second request does not cancel the first. Handler-abort7389–7415 uses twenty bounded6MiB uploads and ignores returned errors, so it is a race/termination scenario rather than success proof. Request-sanitization7417–7434 prohibits an injected header but ignores Do errors and explicitly skips HTTP2 pending an upstream issue, limiting cross-protocol assertions. Proxy-auth7436–7467 checks synthetic URL-escaped credentials at a local proxy; it configures a process-local test environment only if executed, which it was not. Request-canceler7469–7529 supplies up to1GiB before a remote-close-induced write error, then checks bookkeeping cleanup: this source is not authorization to run a large workload.

Drain matrix7531–7668 checks concurrent early Close/EOF, truncation UnexpectedEOF, Close after prior read error, and whether a subsequent request can reuse the connection. It expressly expects Close not to repeat the earlier Read error, so nil Close cannot erase a prior body failure. Trailer test7670–7722 requires automatic drain to consume framing but leave Response.Trailer unpopulated, preserving ownership; this strongly corroborates the implementation distinction from application EOF/checksum validation. ## Concrete header predicates and environment proxy exclusions — 2026-10-18

Full Go SDK vendored reads: src/vendor/golang.org/x/net/http/httpguts/httplex.go1–348 and httpproxy/proxy.go1–374. These exact SDK-vendored bytes, not an independently selected x/net module release, are appended to the [standard-library ledger](stdlib-queue.json). Header names require nonempty ASCII token bytes196–205. Field values303–311 reject control bytes except horizontal tab, including CR/LF/NUL/DEL rejection, while permitting leading/trailing space/tab and opaque high bytes. Host validation209–227 intentionally checks an allowed-byte table rather than full URI/DNS grammar; empty input passes. PunycodeHostPort324–347 returns ASCII input unchanged and delegates non-ASCII conversion to unread IDNA code. Thus the ordinary Transport's reviewed predicates disprove a simplistic CRLF injection inference from Header.Set alone, but do not establish field-specific semantic validity, endpoint scope or trustworthy Unicode display.

Proxy Config copies environment-derived settings into a preprocessed snapshot; uppercase nonempty values take precedence over lowercase90–124. proxyForURL selects by request scheme and explicitly errors for HTTP_PROXY under CGI conditions. init207–214 silently ignores proxy parse errors rather than surfacing them, despite broader comments suggesting errors are returned. Invalid configuration may consequently select no proxy; no real process configuration was inspected or tested. NO_PROXY parsing is best-effort: wildcard bypasses all, literal IP/CIDR checks apply to literal-address requests, and domain entries match hostnames with optional ports. It does not resolve DNS to decide whether a hostname belongs to a CIDR. localhost and literal loopback bypass the environment proxy170–188. This is routing preference behavior, not an egress allowlist, containment guarantee or authorization boundary. A tool can use a direct connection because of these rules even when an operator expects a configured proxy to observe all traffic; actual engagement configuration must supply its own evidence. No proxy function or DNS lookup ran here.

At881–889 transport retry also permits an idle-server-close error after the prior replayability guards; other errors conservatively stop. RegisterProtocol894–943 transfers semantics to a supplied implementation and requires special interfaces for HTTP/2/3. Pool logic1124–1368 checks broken/expired connections, bounds idle counts and evicts LRU entries; HTTP/2 connections are shared while HTTP/1 connections leave the idle pool during use. At1591–1596 the dial deliberately retains context values but detaches request cancellation/deadline so a later request may use it. TLS1781–1827 clones config, supplies ServerName when unset, performs HandshakeContext and closes the connection on failure; handshake-timeout handling waits for the handshake worker after closing. Custom TLS dial paths1886–1918 have different contracts; connection-state availability is not the same as independently verified certificate evidence. Proxy handling1936–2050 distinguishes SOCKS target negotiation, HTTP absolute-form forwarding and HTTPS CONNECT followed by origin TLS. HTTPS proxy plus HTTPS target nests TLS. CONNECT has a one-minute timeout and bounded response-header reader; cancellation closes the connection and waits for its worker. Proxy200 is a tunnel establishment response, not origin authorization or anonymity. Concrete SOCKS and httpproxy dependencies remain unread.

Connection keys2155–2237 include proxy URL, target scheme, target address and HTTP/1 restriction, except plain HTTP via an HTTP(S) proxy deliberately shares a first-hop connection across target hosts. Peer-address telemetry may therefore identify the proxy, not the ultimate origin. HTTP/3 requires a separately supplied implementation and exclusive protocol configuration1836–1857; symbols/interfaces here do not establish that default S3 uses QUIC. Negotiated HTTP/2 switches to another implementation2052–2118, whose framing/flow-control tests are not credited by this file.

Response loop2454–2651 enforces header read limits, then explicitly sets effectively unlimited body reads2526. When transport-added gzip is decoded, Content-Encoding and Content-Length are removed and ContentLength becomes unknown2598–2604. This changes the evidence representation; decompressed bytes cannot be equated with captured compressed wire bytes. Early response Close signals the loop, which may asynchronously drain at most256KiB plus a probe and wait50ms2427–2447 before abandoning reuse. It discards trailer publication while draining to avoid races. This confirms bounded transport reuse cleanup, while leaving outer SDK checksum validation unperformed if its own Read was skipped. Closing is not proof of checksum verification or full application consumption.

readResponse2690–2757 aggregates informational responses under the header bound unless a user Got1xxResponse callback receives them, in which case that callback assumes limiting responsibility and the bound resets. Protocol101 returns a writable connection owned by the caller; it is not ordinary immutable response evidence. writeLoop2830–2865 prioritizes body read errors and counts socket writes for retry decisions. roundTrip2994–3142 writes concurrently with waiting for response; a response may arrive while upload remains in progress. ResponseHeaderTimeout starts after successful request write, so it does not bound a stalled supplied upload body. Explicit context/overall timeout is a separate constraint.

bodyEOFSignal3250–3303 records terminal read errors and makes Close idempotent, allowing transport reuse only under its response-loop conditions. gzipReader3306–3415 manages pooled decompressors and rejects concurrent reads; it forwards Close to the framed body and has no decompressed-byte cap. Gzip header decoding can block on underlying input, so it releases its mutex during initialization and reconciles concurrent close afterward. The final TLS clone/LRU helpers3431–3489 preserve configuration copying and bookkeeping, not independent identity checks. Upstream test files, crypto/tls, bundled HTTP/2, MIME/header primitives and decompressor implementations remain accessible follow-up dependencies; this milestone is not universal exhaustion.

Full read: Go1.27.2 src/net/http/request.go1–1610 in eight contiguous bounded outputs. Request fields explicitly distinguish URL.Host destination from optional Host header override and client-ignored RemoteAddr/TLS fields. Clone391–418 clones maps/URL but shares Body, confirming why request cloning is not a body snapshot. Body's contract186–188 requires concurrent Close to unblock a blocked Read; custom wrappers need reconciliation against that contract rather than an assumption that any io.ReadCloser satisfies it.

Invalid Host header bytes are replaced with empty Host on the direct-write path, while proxy mode errors. These are syntax protections rather than host authorization. Content-Length in the Header map is excluded99–106 and recomputed through transfer fields: the earlier SDK header-only unit test retaining1234 is therefore not proof that1234 is emitted. User-Agent newlines become spaces in direct serialization; general header validation and round-trip method validation still require transport review. The direct Request.Write path contains a TODO for method validation, so distinguish it from NewRequestWithContext's token validation899–911 and from Client.Transport.

NewRequestWithContext933–974 assigns GetBody only for particular concrete in-memory reader types and preserves their backing bytes/snapshots, not an immutable content guarantee under external mutation. Wrapping a reader changes the type switch outcome. Smithy's Build constructs/wraps its request separately, so automatic standard constructor GetBody generation must not be assumed for SDK requests. Zero ContentLength with nonnil ordinary Body becomes unknown at1579–1586; nil/NoBody becomes zero. isReplayable1561–1575 checks body recreation plus selected methods or presence of an idempotency header, not whether a remote service really implements idempotency.

The rest of the file remains relevant parser-boundary evidence: ReadRequest supports HTTP/1 only, validates request line/version, rejects multiple Host values and uses absolute URL authority before Host. Its low-level public readRequest supplies effectively unbounded header-count/memory limits; Server's separate limits are not established here. CONNECT authority parsing is representation handling, not an authenticated tunnel or permitted destination. MaxBytesReader is an explicit opt-in sticky limit, while URL-encoded POST parsing has a10MiB fallback cap; neither caps arbitrary S3 response/error/dump bodies. Multipart parsing delegates memory/disk behavior to mime/multipart; FormValue/PostFormValue intentionally ignore parse errors. Those are additional parser/cleanup dependencies, not newly reviewed implementations. No constructors, parsers, uploads, tests or network operations were executed.

## HTTP/1 transfer framing: contrary evidence to unchecked length — 2026-10-18

Full read: Go1.27.2 src/net/http/transfer.go1–1184 in six contiguous outputs. Writer79–157 derives a sanitized body/length/transfer-encoding triple; nil body with nonzero declared length errors. Trailer names/values pass validateHeaders before emission, and reserved framing trailer names are rejected. This is an actual downstream check absent from trusted SDK header helpers; validateHeaders implementation/caller coverage remains in the unread transport file.

At345–415 writeBody limits bytes sent to a known content length, then drains remaining input to Discard and compares total bytes consumed against the declared length. Short and excess bodies therefore produce an error in this HTTP/1 writer rather than silently accepting arbitrary mismatches. Critically, detection can occur after declared bytes were written; it does not establish that a remote operation was rolled back or never accepted. Extra-body draining has no explicit byte cap here and may block on a supplied reader. A deferred close covers the body writer's failures; original-source closure still depends on wrappers. Chunked and unknown-length branches differ and must not inherit the known-length conclusion. HTTP/2 framing is a separate unread path.

The body probe216–256 waits200ms for a one-byte asynchronous read on normally bodyless methods, then defers that read's result into the eventual stream. That bounds the initial wait, not the supplied reader's lifetime. Copy errors preserve bodyReadError. The transfer reader499–614 selects chunked, bounded-length or close-delimited body readers; unknown-length responses can remain unbounded. It supports only one chunked Transfer-Encoding field on HTTP/1.1 and rejects other/multiple encodings639–663. fixLength669–750 rejects differing duplicate Content-Length fields, deduplicates equal values, validates numeric length and removes Content-Length when chunked framing takes precedence. These are source protections, not a request-smuggling test result across arbitrary intermediaries.

At860–905 premature EOF with remaining LimitedReader bytes becomes UnexpectedEOF; normal exact exhaustion can return data plus EOF. Trailer parsing930–976 bounds delimiter search by the underlying bufio buffer and also applies header-count limits. Automatic drain can parse but deliberately avoid publishing trailers to a response object it no longer owns. Close1003–1047 may consume to EOF, skip when connection closes, or apply early-close limits depending on flags; transport wrappers choose the actual client behavior. None of this invokes an outer S3 checksum wrapper's Read when that wrapper merely forwards Close, so it does not overturn the earlier checksum-close-versus-validation distinction. Content-length parsing1093–1113 allows an explicit compatibility debug setting for empty length; no setting was changed here. No parser, reader, tests or networking code ran.

## Diagnostic dump dependency: buffering and replay — 2026-10-18

Full read: Go1.27.2 src/net/http/httputil/dump.go1–337. drainBody25–37 reads the entire reader into bytes.Buffer, then closes it and returns two readers sharing the captured bytes. Read/close failure returns the original reader with its possibly advanced state, not guaranteed rollback. This resolves Smithy's logging memory-bound question: enabled body dumping has no explicit size cap in the reviewed flow. A caller's body logging is therefore active consumption/buffering and stream replacement, not passive packet observation. No dumping function ran here.

DumpRequestOut77–167 uses a fake connection and actual HTTP Transport serialization, changes HTTPS to HTTP only for this local fake transport and emits a synthetic204 response. This path does not itself dial the real destination. Header-only dumping can nevertheless allocate a dummy body proportional to a positive declared content length before slicing it off; the source acknowledges this. Unknown negative length is fed to LimitReader and does not imply an infinite dummy stream. Pipe readers/writers and idle fake connections receive deferred cleanup and a quit channel handles transport-error reader exit. These are source cleanup mechanisms, not proof every supplied reader terminates.

DumpRequest201–284 explicitly documents approximation: original header order/case are lost, and HTTP/2 is represented as HTTP/1.x. DumpResponse304–335 consumes/replaces the body when enabled and restores ContentLength after serialization; header-only mode substitutes a sentinel reader and suppresses its deliberate error. No redaction or evidence provenance is added here. A dump can support content inspection but is not a byte-identical TLS/HTTP2 wire capture, proof of remote receipt or a replayability guarantee after an arbitrary read failure. Response.Write and underlying Transport remain separate unread boundaries.

Full reads from the already-present portable Go1.27.2 SDK: VERSION1–3, LICENSE1–28 and src/net/http/client.go1–1071 in five contiguous bounded outputs. At504–509 the custom CheckRedirect replaces the default function; only defaultCheckRedirect845–849 imposes ten hops. SDK limitedRedirect has no hop-count check. With the SDK's zero default total timeout, a sequence of permitted307/308 responses is not bounded by that default ten-hop rule; context cancellation, external deadlines, body replay eligibility and transport errors remain constraints. At772–843 each redirect copies headers from the original request snapshot. The sensitive list includes Authorization, WWW-Authenticate, Cookie/Cookie2 and proxy-auth headers, but neither AWS session-token header. SDK's own callback removes X-Amz-Security-Token only when the immediately prior URL.Host differs. Consequently a source trace A→B→B gives a restoration candidate: first cross-host hop deletes the standard token, then the next same-B hop re-copies it from the original snapshot and the SDK callback does not delete it. X-Amz-S3session-Token has no removal in either reviewed layer. These are precise source hypotheses, not runtime receipts or proof of real service credential exposure; actual redirect origin control, token-bearing caller reachability, service acceptance, endpoint constraints and deployed versions remain open. No exploit or network reproduction was attempted.

At700–705 the standard sensitive-header removal becomes sticky after an untrusted hop. At1017–1061 trust uses normalized hostname equality/subdomain relation, not scheme or port, as the client docs explicitly acknowledge. That rule is about selected header forwarding, not assessment scope or identity trust. Redirects occur inside Client.Do below SDK signing, so these files show no per-hop SDK auth resolution/re-signing. At725–729 redirect cleanup reads at most2KiB before closing the prior response; ErrUseLastResponse returns it unclosed for caller ownership. At92–107 a configured client timeout includes body reading, while client metrics ending at Do do not. At997–1014 the timer-body Read does not call stop even on EOF despite its preceding comment; Close does, reinforcing explicit close ownership. Header-copy dependencies and transport wire/body enforcement remain unread selected files, not blanket conclusions about every protocol.

## S3 auth selection, identity failure and anonymous fallback — 2026-10-18

Full reads: S3 auth.go1–394, auth_scheme_test.go1–160, endpoint_auth_resolver.go1–127 and endpoint_auth_resolver_test.go1–135. Resolution binds operation/region/endpoint inputs, obtains ordered options, then selects the first supported option with a nonnil resolver; an explicit anonymous option is immediately selectable. Preferences reorder options, and bare scheme names may match the suffix of a registered namespaced ID. Defaults model V4 then V4a; WriteGetObjectResponse adds unsigned-payload signaling. Endpoint auth options, when present, are authoritative with modeled properties merged underneath endpoint values. The resolver canonicalizes the express identifier and explicitly appends anonymous for compatibility.

Important contrary evidence: this is not a retry-on-bad-credentials downgrade. After selecting a configured resolver, GetIdentity errors return directly; the code does not fall through to anonymous after credential retrieval failure. Signing similarly requires resolved scheme, nonnil identity and signer and returns signer errors without trying another scheme. Anonymous can be selected when no earlier matching resolver is configured, or a trusted preference/resolver orders it first. Neither successful selection nor a nil no-op signature proves service permissions. The service still decides whether unsigned access is allowed.

Options and properties are shallowly carried between resolution stages; endpoint option pointers are modified by rebase/canonicalization. Custom resolver reuse/concurrency needs the underlying Properties and resolver contract reviewed before claiming safe isolation or a race. Missing endpoint Region is intentionally stubbed to empty for compatibility with custom/legacy resolution, rather than proving an endpoint region was validated. Legacy context signing overrides apply only to V4/V4a IDs. Tests exercise a mock selection matrix and endpoint signing-property merge, not actual identity failures, express credentials, redirects or cryptographic requests. No tests or service calls ran. Redirect follow-up remains below signing in the HTTP client; concrete standard-library header copying is still unread and prevents a disclosure conclusion.

## Legacy Content-MD5 and response wrappers — 2026-10-18

Full reads: transport/http/checksum_middleware.go1–71, checksum_middleware_test.go1–86, md5_checksum.go1–26 and response.go1–35. This separate legacy Build middleware skips any nonempty supplied Content-MD5 without verifying it. For a nonnil stream it requires seekability, hashes from current position to EOF using io.Copy, then rewinds to the request's recorded starting position before installing Base64 MD5. Read failures return before rewind, leaving stream position changed; rewind failures return without installing the computed header. There is no explicit length limit or context-aware read loop in this helper. These are stream-ownership/error semantics, not a demonstrated reachable cloud exploit. MD5 here is a content checksum, not an identity/signature or authorization mechanism, and must not be conflated with the separately reviewed S3 flexible checksum dependency.

Tests assert nil versus explicit empty body behavior, one abc digest, unseekable rejection and resulting StreamLength. They do not consume/recompare the resulting stream, supply a preexisting invalid digest or cover read/rewind failure recovery. No tests ran. ResponseError merely stores a response and nested error, exposes status/response and unwraps; its status/error accessors assume nonnil response. The wrapper supplies status evidence, not body validity, credential provenance or successful operation semantics.

## Header comment helper boundary — 2026-10-18

Full reads: transport/http/middleware_header_comment.go1–82 and middleware_header_comment_test.go1–114. Trusted stack configuration collects case-insensitive comment strings; during Build it reads Header.Get and, if nonempty, replaces that field with the first retrieved value plus parenthesized comments. Content is inserted as-is without escaping parentheses/backslashes or verifying whether the selected field permits comments; the latter limitation is expressly documented. Actual wire rejection of invalid control characters remains transport-dependent, not established here. Tests exercise case-insensitive lookup, absent-header no-op and comment order, with single-valued starting headers; they do not establish multi-value preservation or comment grammar validity. ## What HTTP telemetry establishes — 2026-10-18

Full reads: transport/http/metrics.go1–205 and metrics_test.go1–166. A concrete NopMeter skips instrumentation; otherwise instrument-creation errors prevent wrapping. httptrace callbacks record DNS, connection and TLS elapsed times, time to first response byte and acquired-connection increments/decrements. The callbacks discard DNS result/error, connection error and TLS state/error arguments. These duration metrics therefore do not prove successful DNS resolution, certificate validation or an authorized destination. Do duration ends when Client.Do returns, not when a streamed response reaches EOF, notwithstanding its entire-transaction description.

Each metric family has a single atomic timestamp slot. Atomic storage avoids a data race on that slot but does not pair overlapping connection attempts individually; transport callback concurrency and reuse semantics remain in the standard-library boundary. Acquired counts increment on GotConn and decrement on PutIdleConn, with no body-close wrapper here; this is callback-derived observability, not a complete leak oracle. The source explicitly declines to track idle connections because available hooks cannot identify them reliably.

The test supplies a mock clock, manually invokes trace hooks in one serial order and checks arithmetic plus an intentionally unreturned acquired connection. It neither exercises an actual request nor asserts callback behavior under HTTP/2 multiplexing, connection failure, concurrent dialing, body close or instrument errors. No test ran. Duration/counter evidence must remain separate from response-integrity, cleanup and scope evidence.

## XXH64 digest input flow

Fully read xxhash.go1–244, xxhash_unsafe.go1–59, xxhash_safe.go1–17, xxhash_asm.go1–16 and xxhash_other.go1–77. At xxhash.go25–66 New/Reset establish a zero seed and clear counters; Reset does not zero the backing buffer, but n=0 makes old bytes outside active state. At74–110 Write appends bytes, completes32-byte blocks and retains a tail. It inserts no separators or semantic fields: equivalent supplied byte streams remain equivalent regardless of Write call boundaries. At112–168 Sum/Sum64 compute from current state without consuming it, using only active tail bytes and total length. That supports the resolver semantic-preimage finding; no hash collision search is needed to observe fields never written by its caller.

At170–220 binary state serialization validates magic/exact size, restores counters and bounds n modulo32; this is not used in the reviewed resolver flow. Arithmetic rounds/tail endian reads at219–243 are deterministic XXH64 operations, not authentication or authorization. No implementation correctness claim against an external specification or published vectors is made from this read alone.

WriteString on ordinary !appengine builds creates an unsafe read-only byte view, then calls Write; safe appengine variant converts to bytes. The active Write copies its buffered tail and processes blocks rather than retaining the source slice, narrowing a caller-string lifetime concern. Build tags route amd64/arm64 gc non-purego to assembly writeBlocks/Sum64 declarations, other builds to pure Go. Assembly and tests remain unread; do not call the active native flow complete or claim installed binary equivalence. Pure Go block/tail branches are bounded by remaining slice lengths. Resolver context/key omissions are the caller's semantics, not a cryptographic break in xxhash.

## Prioritized unread queue

Assembly/test closure: xxhash_amd64.s1–210 and xxhash_arm64.s1–184 fully read, not assembled/run. Both implement block rounds, length-qualified tails and final mixing; writeBlocks assumes at least32 bytes, supplied by the reviewed Digest.Write branch. ## jsonschema compiler and resource boundary

Source only, v6.0.3, 2026-10-18 UTC: compiler.go1–333, loader.go1–267 and roots.go1–287 completely read. NewCompiler creates per-instance compiled-schema/resource maps and defaults format/content assertions off; draft-specific format rules still apply later. AddResource rejects duplicate URLs and built-in metaschema replacement. Compile resolves a fragment, queues referenced schemas and publishes compiled schema entries only after its queue succeeds; resource-loader state can already have changed, so this is not a transaction over all compiler state. Empty object schemas compile to true. No synchronization or queue-size budget appears in these files. Following objcompiler/draft/validator remains necessary before claiming enum/type enforcement.

The actual default loader is FileLoader (roots.go17–27), not an automatic HTTP client. Built-in json-schema.org metaschemas are embedded; unknown resources delegate to that file loader unless the application installs another loader. Thus a URL-looking schema identifier is not evidence of remote fetching. FileLoader opens the converted file path and defers close; there is no confinement or byte limit in that loader, but untrusted schema selection by BloodHound is not established and its reviewed caller supplies embedded schemas. Do not convert this capability into an application path-read finding. UnmarshalJSON uses json.Number and rejects trailing top-level content by requiring EOF after the first decoded value, narrowing a trailing-garbage concern.

roots.go collects dialect resources and anchors, rejects duplicate IDs, tracks processed subschemas, follows draft/vocabulary-selected child schemas and validates schema documents against their metaschema. ensureSubschema clones and validates before replacing the root. Custom metaschema traversal has a URL-cycle check. Those checks describe schema compilation, not evidence that instance data corresponds to a real directory object or that cache keys encode the same predicate. Remaining dependency files and tests stay unread in the queue.

## Instance keyword enforcement and limits

objcompiler.go1–554 and validator.go1–978 fully read, source only. The compiler materializes type/enum/required/minimum-length and array/object restrictions under the active dialect vocabulary, and enqueues conditional branches and references. In pre2019 schemas a dollar-ref suppresses other keywords; 2019+ does not. Description/default/readOnly/writeOnly/examples are stored annotations, not transformations or access-control checks. The intVal helper converts arbitrary rational integer to int via Int64 without an explicit range guard here; the fixed embedded application schemas use small values, so user-controlled constraint overflow is not established.

Validate creates fresh traversal/error/unevaluated state. Types reject incompatible values; enum checks supported type then equality against its values; required checks presence, not non-null or nonempty by itself. minItems0 accepts an empty array, independently confirming why an application schema description cannot establish a nonempty-kind invariant. Patterns use MatchString (substring semantics unless anchored); string lengths count Unicode runes. oneOf rejects multiple matches; if success selects then and failure selects else. Unevaluated tracking merges only successful child evaluations. Unknown or omitted business invariants are not invented by this engine.

Source shows cycle detection for revisiting the same schema pointer at the same value depth, not a general byte/depth/error budget. Nested values increase depth; error causes, contains-match lists and unevaluated maps can scale with instance data. Full mode accumulates causes; boolResult branches use empty ValidationError and selected short-circuit paths, not a global cancellation mechanism. This cannot make the application cap of15 reported validation errors an object-memory limit. No runtime resource-exhaustion claim or experiment was made. equals/typeOf/duplicates implementations, dialect definitions, output localization and tests remain queued; source-reading these callers is not proof those dependencies are correct.

## Equality and duplicate detection: contrary evidence

util.go1–468 and util_test.go1–112 fully read, unrun. Equality recursively compares object key sets and array order, separates strings from numbers, and compares supported numeric representations with big.Rat. Arrays of20 or fewer use pairwise equality; larger arrays bucket on a randomized maphash then still compare equality inside each bucket. Therefore a structural hash collision here does not itself establish a false duplicate: unlike the reviewed graph resolver, this implementation retains an equality check. The hash encoding omits some structural delimiters and numeric sign in magnitude bytes, but that only enlarges buckets for valid inputs under the reviewed algorithm; do not report hash collision as acceptance bypass.

writeHash returns InvalidJsonValue for unsupported types, yet recursive calls and duplicates ignore that return; malformed json.Number can leave a nil rational before Num. Those are API-domain candidates requiring typeOf and caller admission review, not established attacks through ordinary encoding/json values. Tests cover selected numeric equivalence and string/number distinction plus fragment escaping, not large-array forced collision, nested invalid values or every numeric domain. JSON pointer lookup bounds checks array indices and rejects invalid tilde escaping. URL/reference resolution and Windows path conversion are parsing capabilities, not network I/O receipts.

## Formats and decoded content

content.go1–52 and format.go1–709 fully read, source only. Built-ins validate syntactic strings (pointer, UUID shape, duration, IP, ASCII hostname/email, dates/times, URI/template and semver). They do not resolve DNS, deliver email, contact a URI, verify ownership or establish routability/scope. Most format functions return nil for nonstrings, requiring a separate type constraint. UUID checks hex groups, not issuance or uniqueness. URI requires an absolute parsed scheme, not a scheme allowlist. Hostname validation accepts syntactic labels, not existence. Period validates endpoint syntax, not temporal ordering. IRI names share URI parser implementation; internationalized-domain/email support is not inferred from the names.

Some syntax routines use strconv.Atoi on fixed fields without an explicit ASCII-digit loop (IPv4 groups and time components), while duration/semver loop digits themselves. This is a source candidate for broader-than-intended lexical acceptance, not a demonstrated standard violation or application bypass; reachability depends on an asserted format in the embedded schemas and comparative specification/fixture review remains queued. URI-template checking inspects RawPath and brace alternation, not a full template expression grammar. Avoid claiming general RFC conformance from comments.

Content assertions default disabled in the reviewed compiler. If enabled, base64 decoder allocates decoded bytes; JSON content Validate uses encoding/json.Unmarshal, whereas contentSchema decoding calls the precision-preserving UnmarshalJSON. No local decoded-byte limit appears here. Neither path executes decoded content. The known application schema/caller must actually opt into assertions before attributing those behaviors to upload admission.

## x/text Unicode decoding implementation

Exact selected module v0.43.0: encoding/unicode/unicode.go1–513, override.go1–83 and utf32/utf32.go1–297 fully read, not executed. UTF8 decoder replaces invalid subsequences with RuneError and can expand input; incomplete source waits with ErrShortSrc before EOF but becomes replacement at EOF. UTF8BOM strips an initial BOM, ordinary UTF8 does not. UTF16 decoder uses default endian unless initial accepted BOM overrides; ExpectBOM errors on missing initial marker. Subsequent markers are data, not repeated endian changes. Unpaired surrogates and odd trailing bytes become replacement rather than fatal parse errors. Reset restores initial policy/state. A helper named isHighSurrogate actually tests DC00–DFFF (the second/low surrogate range); interpret its numeric predicate and DecodeRune call, not the misleading identifier.

BOMOverride recognizes only UTF8/16, waits for3bytes before nonEOF decision, and switches UTF8 BOM input to transform.Nop rather than UTF8 replacement decoder. Therefore it is not automatically equivalent to UTF8BOM on malformed bytes following a marker. Its applicability to the BloodHound BOM wrapper requires the already reviewed caller path, not name matching. UTF32 separately checks initial4byte marker, replaces invalid scalar values/trailing incomplete units at EOF, and preserves state until reset. No decoder proves raw-byte preservation; normalized text may intentionally differ. Transformer transport/error propagation, UTF8 tables/runes dependencies and exact tests remain queued.

UTF32 narrowing: an initial nonempty1–3byte input while acceptBOM is still set returns ErrShortSrc even at EOF (utf32.go186–189), before the trailing replacement loop. Replacement of1–3trailing bytes applies after initial policy resolution or IgnoreBOM. Do not generalize the later EOF loop to every short initial input. Reader/transform behavior determines the surfaced error; queued next.

## Transformer reader/writer and resource bounds

transform/transform.go1–710 fully read, unrun. NewReader resets its transformer and allocates separate4096byte source/destination buffers. It processes returned bytes before read errors, passes atEOF only for io.EOF, preserves a nonEOF underlying read error over a transform error, and surfaces ErrShortSrc when EOF prevents completing an initial UTF32 marker. Thus decoding does not silently convert every truncated source into replacement. Progressing ErrShortDst drains output; full source/no-progress cannot grow these fixed buffers indefinitely. The wrapper has no context or Close method and does not close the underlying input, so lifecycle/cancellation must come from the owning upload code. Repeated underlying0,nil reads have no explicit no-progress counter here.

Writer buffers incomplete source until more Write or Close. Close flushes transformer with atEOF but does not close its underlying writer. It delegates write-error signaling and does not inspect a short count with nil error; conforming io.Writer must supply an error in that case. No durable storage/sync receipt follows from transform.Close. Chain uses4096byte intermediate buffers per link, propagates fatal errors after downstream accepted bytes, and detects selected nonprogress/full-buffer conditions; this is not a global input quota. Reset resets links/buffer offsets, while err/errStart handling depends on completed Transform error drain; interrupted reuse/tests need review before declaring a reset bug.

String/Bytes/Append reset state and grow destination buffers as needed; unlike Reader streaming, these convenience APIs materialize full output and expose no maximum allocation quota. Span may mutate state and is not a read-only validation predicate. Nop copies bytes without UTF8 checking, confirming the BOMOverride UTF8 branch distinction. Caller must account for nDst/nSrc before err and retain a separate raw artifact when exact original bytes matter. Tests and downstream encoding wrappers remain unread.

encoding/encoding.go1–336 fully read, source only. Decoder.Bytes/String discard partial output on error, while raw transform APIs return partial progress; Reader streams it. Encoder.Writer is typed io.Writer even though the concrete transform.Writer supports Close, so a caller must retain/access the closer to flush. Decoder/encoder reuse while wrappers remain active is explicitly prohibited by comments and shared state. Nop preserves malformed bytes; UTF8Validator rejects the first invalid sequence (or asks for more source before EOF), so successful replacement-decoding is not strict UTF8 validation. Legacy unsupported-rune handlers can deliberately produce replacement/HTML entity text, with documented ambiguity; no caller reachability inferred.

## Conditional rune flow and tests

runes/cond.go1–188, cond_test.go1–283, runes_test.go1–665 and transform/examples_test.go1–38 fully read, unrun. If classifies malformed input as RuneError for choosing a branch but forwards original malformed bytes to that branch.
