## S3 manager upload entry through completion

Exact manager v1.22.22 upload.go1–998 fully read, source only. Defaults are5MiB parts and5workers per Upload, while the reviewed BloodHound wrapper overrides8MiB/4workers. Defaults are not application settings. The pinned manager declares deprecation in favor of transfermanager; this is an artifact statement, not a current online migration recommendation. Uploader is copied per call, but its input pointer remains shared and multipart initChecksumAlgorithm mutates input.ChecksumAlgorithm. Concurrent reuse of one input requires separate review; constructor concurrency safety does not make arbitrary shared caller inputs immutable.

Seekable input size is measured to enlarge part size and calculate expected part count; nonseekable first part is io.ReadAll of a LimitReader(partSize), later parts use pooled buffers. LimitReader is per part, not a total-byte quota. Reads are synchronous without a context-aware wrapper here; canceled pool acquisition/API calls do not necessarily interrupt a blocked input.Read. readFillBuf has no explicit0,nil read bound. Known-size ReaderAt sections support independent part reads; correctness depends on the underlying content staying stable. Negative/oversized option validation and pool behavior need dependency/tests review before impact claims.

Multipart creates an upload, schedules numbered chunks, workers append completion records under a mutex and always run chunk cleanup. Worker errors do not cancel the shared context locally; queued chunks drain with cleanup and no further send when error exists. Error setter overwrites earlier errors, so returned cause need not be the first chronological failure. No upload-source Close occurs in this file. Single-part calls PutObject and reports SDK metadata. Multipart sorts records and calls CompleteMultipartUpload, then checks expected count afterward. A count mismatch can therefore return an error after a successful complete side effect; fail attempts abort, which is not evidence an already completed object was rolled back. Unknown-size inputs skip expected-count validation. This is source ordering, not a reproduced object-store defect.

Abort uses the original context and ignores its returned error; canceled-context cleanup is not independently assured. LeavePartsOnError intentionally skips abort. Returned MultiUploadFailure retains uploadID but no abort success receipt. Checksums and ETag come from SDK operation responses; Location strips request query but is not an independent readback/hash receipt. SDK serialization, retries/checksum enforcement, service semantics and mocks remain queued. No S3 client, cloud account, network call, credentials or multipart operation was executed.

## Upload buffer pool and API boundary

pool.go1–252, api.go1–38 and arn.go1–24 fully read, unrun. Pool Get checks cancellation before allocation/availability and while waiting; releases its read lock while awaiting capacity changes to avoid the documented writer/read-lock deadlock. Allocation tokens bound pooled slices for the current capacity, but concurrent uploads add capacity and the first nonseekable part is allocated separately. This is not a global process-memory quota. Put drops buffers when capacity is zero/full; it does not zero bytes. Close drains references, not cryptographic erasure. Returned per-upload capacity wrapper reduces only its recorded positive delta. Negative cumulative capacity has no guard before channel creation in this file; caller option reach/tests determine whether this is a practical configuration failure. Fixed application concurrency4 avoids inferring untrusted control.

UploadAPIClient is an injected interface for five operations; mocks can satisfy it without transport. ARN validation rejects s3-object-lambda service ARNs but is not an assessment authorization/scope policy, account ownership check or endpoint egress allowlist. The parser dependency itself remains unread. No API implementation or credentials inferred from the interface.

## Platform upload buffering and read semantics

read_seeker_write_to.go1–66, buffered_read_seeker.go1–80, buffered_read_seeker_test.go1–80 and platform default files1–8/1–6 fully read, unrun. NonWindows default buffer provider is nil; Windows filename-selected implementation defaults a1MiB sync.Pool buffer. This affects known ReaderAtSeeker sections, not every input. Cleanup returns buffers without erasing them. Buffered reader fills on demand, then copies to smaller caller output; Seek delegates directly and resets buffered indices. ReadAt uses Seek then Read, changes shared cursor and can return a short successful read; callers need interface-contract and reachability review before assuming concurrency-safe ReaderAt semantics.

If an underlying fill returns bytes with an error, Read can return the error with only the smaller caller portion copied while more bytes remain buffered; a consumer stopping on that error may omit buffered remainder. Existing tests use bytes.Reader and do not supply a combined final-bytes-plus-EOF reader, seek-current after read-ahead, failing seeks or concurrent ReadAt. These are precise source candidates, not reproduced upload truncation. Normal section/file reader behavior and SDK transport read sizes may narrow reachability. Tests assert selected bytes for reads/absolute seek/ReadAt but ignore ReadAt count. No test execution.

- **`middleware_add.go:1–176`, complete:** input checksum setup and removal, then output context registration and supported-algorithm filtering. Output options describe using the first available algorithm, but the consumer loop below does not implement a first-match break. Registration is before/after relative to middleware steps, not a guarantee that every response is validated.
- **`middleware_validate_output.go:1–136`, complete:** propagates downstream errors; skips unless context mode is enabled; validates only HTTP **200**, so a 206 ranged response takes the skip path. Iterates all configured supported algorithms and overwrites its selection on every present header: **last present algorithm wins**, contrary to the first-algorithm comment. Multipart skip checks `strings.Contains(expectedChecksum, "-")`, not a parsed numeric suffix. Missing headers and skipped multipart checks can return success without a validation reader. Metadata records the algorithm selected for future validation, not a completed validation receipt.
- **`algorithms.go:1–180`:** supported list contains CRC32C, CRC32, SHA1, SHA256, CRC64NVME and SHA512. Filtering preserves supplied order and removes duplicates; MD5 and XXHASH variants supplied by the S3 operation are absent from this module's supported list. Distinguish a modeled checksum output from actual supported validation. MD5 exists separately as a request-body helper, which does not put it in this response-validation set.
- **`algorithms.go:181–345`, file complete:** compute reader records a checksum only on EOF and retains non-EOF errors. Validation reader hashes bytes as they are read, compares only when a read returns EOF, and returns a mismatch from that read. **Close only forwards to the original body; it neither drains nor validates.** Consequently reading a prefix or closing early cannot establish content integrity. Validation compares Base64 strings with `strings.EqualFold`; Base64 is case-sensitive, so this is a source-level comparison discrepancy requiring tests and caller/transport reachability analysis before any impact claim. No exploit, runtime reproduction or remote behavior is asserted.

These observations supersede any shorthand implying that all ten modeled algorithms are validated, that first-listed always wins, or that a successful GetObject call/Close establishes validation. ### Checksum input computation and retry state (2026-10-18)

`checksum/middleware_compute_input_checksum.go:1–460` complete, source only. **1–230:** metadata exposes the stored map directly. Any existing header with the checksum prefix bypasses computation, without validating its value or supported algorithm in this branch; metadata can therefore represent a supplied checksum despite the computed name. Header-map iteration chooses an arbitrary matching entry when several exist. Otherwise context algorithm parsing enforces the six supported names. HTTPS, non-presigning, nonempty streaming input can select trailers; non-trailer nonempty input must be seekable. Header checksums and optional SHA256 are cached on the middleware across attempts; rewind occurs only when determined length is positive. Cache validity depends on body stability and per-operation middleware lifetime, not verified bytes on every attempt. Fresh operation stacks previously reviewed narrow cross-operation sharing; custom interceptors/body mutation and tests remain to follow.

**231–460:** trailer middleware activates only from stack context, requires HTTPS and nonempty stream, and independently bypasses any existing checksum header. It wraps the stream, adds encoding headers, copies request via SetStream and sets encoded length; known original length may become a decoded-content-length header. Cached checksum may be reused in trailers; available checksum is cached even after a failed attempt. On downstream success, lack of EOF-derived checksum becomes an error **after** downstream handling, so that error alone cannot prove the service did not accept a write. Input hashing uses io.Copy without a local byte/time cap; seekable/source/transport bounds remain caller obligations. Stream length trusts nonnegative ContentLength first. Final HandleMiddleware error is ignored at 1217, although inner attempt assertions check failure/success. First-attempt error text field is unused in this retry test. #### Actual S3 retry placement and shared stream

Re-read already complete `s3/api_client.go:750–800,1310–1372`: S3 creates core Attempt with Smithy RequestCloner and places it **before ResolveAuthScheme**, so auth/endpoint/checksum work can be within attempts. BeforeRetryLoop interceptors sit before Retry; attempt interceptors after Retry. Trusted interceptors are optional and no default mutation is established. Precise later ordering follows insertion semantics and remaining middleware, not textual registration sequence alone.

`smithy/transport/http/request.go:1–189` complete: Clone copies the stream reference, not bytes; HTTP fields are cloned. StreamLength prefers Len, otherwise seeks to end and restores the recorded initial position. Rewind returns there, respecting user-provided initial offset; it does not snapshot file contents. SetStream captures seek position and returns a clone, recognizes NoBody and nonseekable known-empty readers. Build wraps stream in NopCloser plus SafeReadCloser, with PipeReader special handling; **transport body closure therefore does not directly close the original source through this wrapper**. Source owner cleanup and safe reader lifecycle are separate. Nonzero declared content length is not validated against reader contents here. These facts narrow checksum retry behavior: default retries share/reseek a body, while external mutation remains possible; no content replacement is performed by the retry loop itself. Next: SafeReadCloser, request tests, core retry tests/token policy. No execution.

`smithy/transport/http/internal/io/safe.go:1–76` and `safe_test.go:1–171` complete, read only. Read/WriteTo hold a mutex through the underlying operation; Close waits for that lock, marks closed, drops reference, forwards once; subsequent reads EOF and closes nil. It prevents wrapper races but **cannot interrupt an indefinitely blocked reader while waiting on the same mutex**. In request Build the underlying closer is a NopCloser, narrowing cleanup effects. Tests cover read/close errors, state/reference clearing, idempotent Close and WriterTo before/after close with literal output. No concurrent/blocking test or cancellation oracle. No deadlock reproduced.

### Standard retry policy narrows token and unbounded-attempt candidates

`sdkcore/aws/retry/standard.go:1–270` complete. Standard defaults to three attempts, max backoff20s, retry quota500, ordinary cost5, timeout10, success increment1; constructor replaces nonpositive MaxAttempts with three. **Thus the generic Attempt loop's nonpositive-unlimited branch is not reached by default Standard construction.** Custom retryers/wrappers remain separate. Retryables/timeouts slices are copied but nested maps/policies remain trusted configurable state. GetAttemptToken does not reserve a concurrency resource: it returns a callback that adds a token only on success. **This rejects a default Standard attempt-token resource-leak inference on rewind failure.** Retry quota is intentionally consumed on failure; releaseToken only refunds for nil error. A canceled-delay exit after acquiring quota may still consume quota for an unsent next attempt; record this accounting boundary, not an unreleased lock or network-resource leak. Concrete adaptive/custom policies remain unread.

### Adaptive retry enforcement, complete implementation files

`sdkcore/aws/retry/adaptive.go:1–157`, `adaptive_ratelimit.go:1–159`, `adaptive_token_bucket.go:1–84` complete, source only. Adaptive wraps NewStandard, so default max remains bounded. Appended already cached exact `aws/protocol/eventstream@v1.7.11`: 39 files (30 text,9 binary fixtures), origin5841d3ae2cfd6e6113ca61b71d69131b84932f4c, tag2026-05-29T18:20:45Z, content sum `h1:h5+3VT69KUBK24grGuuA5saDJTj2IIjLb9au668Fo5I=`. `decode.go:1–219` complete: checks prelude and message CRC32; reads headers/payload through declared-length LimitReaders; payload grows bytes.Buffer via io.Copy, logging can tee an additional raw-message buffer and render hex/JSON. CRC proves framing consistency under that checksum, not authentication, completeness of query results or a memory/time bound. **`message.go:1–100` complete:** PayloadLen computes uint32 Length-HeadersLen-16; ValidateLens rejects only Length==0, not Length<16, HeadersLen>available, or an explicit maximum frame/header size. Thus nonzero malformed declarations can underflow to a large bounded uint32 payload demand, and the10KiB caller buffer is not a cap. Prelude CRC still must match; a conforming authenticated service/custom transport/caller remains the source boundary. This is an unresolved parser/resource-bound candidate, **not observed allocation/exploit or a demonstrated AWS service attack**. io.Copy's short input ends before demanded bytes, and later CRC read may fail; it is not an immediate preallocation of4GiB. Need header parser/test/transport constraints next.

Message.Clone copies payload and delegates header cloning; rawMessage encoding narrows lengths to uint32 without local size guard here. Unknown frame cloning adds memory proportional to decoded content. No malformed frame was generated or decoded in this research.

`eventstream/header.go:1–176` complete: name bound255 from uint8/fixed array; value type dispatch rejects unknown type. Headers are case-sensitive and Set replaces first exact name, so decoded duplicate names collapse to last value; linear Set per header yields potentially quadratic work with many unique declared headers, without local count cap. Clone copies Value interfaces, **not underlying mutable BytesValue storage**; deep-copy comment requires narrowing by value implementation. Del loop can skip adjacent duplicates in manually constructed slices, though decoder Set prevents such duplicates. No remote duplicate/injection claim.

`decode_test.go:1–188` complete, not run: positive fixtures compare decoded headers/payload/re-encoded lengths/CRC; negatives require some error without checking declared error kind. ### Trusted interceptor contracts and actual middleware semantics

`smithy/transport/http/interceptor.go:1–322` complete: registry Copy copies slices of interface values, **not interceptor instances or their internal state**, narrowing deep-copy language. Docs prohibit replacing InterceptorContext fields but allow mutating pointed values; no base interceptors installed. This makes mutation a trusted extension capability, not a default attacker control. Documented error location determines retry participation; after-signing mutation can invalidate signature.

`interceptor_middleware.go:1–326` complete. **1–165:** one shared context initialized per execution; Input set, Request assigned after serialization. BeforeExecution error returns before AfterExecution, contradicting the interface's unconditional after-everything wording; not an exception/panic-safe finally mechanism. After hooks can overwrite original returned error and stop remaining hooks. **166–326:** transmission response assigned only after next succeeds; deserialization hooks skip RequestSendError specifically, otherwise can run with partial state. Attempt before error skips after, consistent with its docs. Request pointer is not refreshed in per-attempt hook here despite retry cloning; response/output fields are not reset per attempt. Thus hooks may observe original/shared request or stale earlier response/output on later failure; establish exact consumers before impact. S3 registration and fresh operation stack previously reviewed narrow cross-operation state; trusted interceptor object state can persist by design. No hooks, mutation, retry or test executed. No test files for these names exist in this pinned inventory; broader integration tests remain queued, so do not call runtime coverage absent globally.

`sdkcore/aws/retry/retryable_error.go:1–229`, `throttle_error.go:1–61`, `retry.go:1–91` complete. Ordered retry classifiers honor first nonunknown; cancellation marker blocks default retry, explicit RetryableError can override later connection/status checks. Connection classifier excludes DNS IsNotFound, accepts selected temporary/dial/timeout or error-text connection reset/refused, recursively unwraps URL/net.OpError. Classification is heuristic policy, not proof remote operation was unperformed/idempotent or target authorized. Throttle classification exact-code matches; no local traffic budget follows solely from an API error string.

Retry wrappers can override max directly with **no positive-value validation** (`AddWithMaxAttempts`), so wrapping Standard with0 reopens generic unlimited-attempt-count behavior previously narrowed for NewStandard alone. Standard quota/context may still bound it; do not call it infinite in every deployment. Added-code wrapper tests its own codes before inner policy and can override a prior cancellation policy for matching errors; context check in Attempt still stops a canceled context before send. RetryerV1 adapter forwards GetInitialToken without context-aware blocking. Trusted custom configuration contracts remain material; no classifiers, retryers or account traffic executed.

`sdkcore/aws/retry/retryable_error_test.go:1–313` complete: wrapped/plain connection text, marker interfaces, status/code nesting, cancellation precedence and DNS cases with exact ternary oracles; all fake error objects, no DNS/socket calls. It distinguishes plain refused unknown from URL-wrapped refused retryable. `retry_test.go:1–38` only tests added codes on NopRetryer, no max0/cancellation composite cases. `metadata.go:1–53`, `attempt_metrics.go:1–52`, `errors.go:1–21` complete: result accessor returns slice-containing struct without deep clone; error unwrap retained; metrics initialization can abort call before attempts and duration is described as through status/headers, **not complete streaming body/validation duration**. Attempt count/retryability records are local state, not service-side idempotency or acceptance evidence. No tests or metrics initialized in research.
